> AGENT

Alright, here's a straight-up assessment based on your numbers.

## Where you stand

**Body composition:** 75.3kg at 170cm with 24.1% body fat puts you in the "average" category for a 26-year-old male. Not overweight, but carrying more fat than muscle. Resting HR of 89 is on the higher side — ideally you'd want 60-70 — which signals low cardiovascular conditioning.

**Upper body strength:** Weak, honestly. 1.5 pull-ups and 11 push-ups at your bodyweight is well below average for your age. Zero ring dips and a 1.5s tucked L-sit confirm this. The silver lining is that you have 3 years of gym history, so muscle memory should help you progress faster than a true beginner.

**Core/endurance strength:** Plank at 58s is decent — not far from the 2-min target. 43 squats is actually solid for lower body endurance. Dead hang at 30s is weak but improvable quickly.

**Cardio:** A ~33-minute 5K is beginner level. Sub-23 is a big jump — you'd be going from ~6:40/km to ~4:35/km pace.

**Flexibility:** Can't touch toes + shoulder imbalance is a red flag for injury if you push hard without addressing it. Deep squat being easy is a good sign though.

## Are the goals achievable?

| Goal | Verdict | Timeline |
|------|---------|----------|
| Pull-ups 10-14 | Very doable | 4-6 months |
| Push-ups 35-45 | Very doable | 3-5 months |
| Plank 2+ min | Easy | 6-8 weeks |
| 5K sub-23:00 | Achievable but hard | 6-12 months |
| 10K sub-48:00 | Harder than the 5K goal | 9-15 months |
| Body fat 12% | Hardest one | 12-18 months, requires diet discipline |

**The strength goals are very realistic.** With muscle memory and consistent training 3-4x/week, pull-ups and push-ups will climb fast. Beginners with gym history often see rapid initial gains.

**The 5K goal is ambitious but doable.** Going from 33:00 to sub-23:00 is shaving 10 minutes — that's a transformation, not a tweak. But at 26 with no injuries, if you run 3x/week with structured training (easy runs + intervals + tempo), it's absolutely within reach in 6-12 months. Getting your body fat down will also make this easier — less weight to carry.

**Body fat 12% is the real grind.** Going from 24% to 12% means losing ~9kg of pure fat while ideally gaining some muscle. This is 80% diet. Training alone won't get you there. Your 12-18 month timeline is realistic if nutrition is locked in.

## Biggest risks

1. **Doing too much too fast** — your Pitta-Vata constitution + currently aggravated Pitta means you'll want to go hard and burn out. Gradual progression is critical.
2. **Ignoring flexibility** — the shoulder imbalance and hamstring tightness will cause problems if you start loading up pull-ups and running volume without mobility work.
3. **Skipping the diet piece** — the body fat goal won't happen through training alone.

Overall? None of your goals are unrealistic. They're just going to take consistent work over months, not weeks. The muscle memory is a real advantage — don't waste it by going too hard and getting injured early.

> DEVELOPER

Really I would've thought that most people can't do a pullup. Why is the bodyfat the hardest one?

> AGENT

You're right that most *general population* can't do a pull-up — but for a 26-year-old male who spent 3 years in the gym, 1.5 is below where you'd expect. It's not alarming, just means you've detrained. The good news is that's exactly why it'll come back fast.

On body fat — it's the hardest because:

1. **It's mostly a diet problem, not a training problem.** You can't out-train a bad diet. Going from 24% to 12% means losing ~9kg of fat, which requires a sustained caloric deficit over many months. Every other goal on your list just requires showing up and training consistently.

2. **It gets exponentially harder as you go lower.** Going from 24% to 18%? Relatively easy — clean up diet, train regularly, it'll happen. Going from 18% to 15%? Requires more discipline. Going from 15% to 12%? Your body actively fights you — hunger increases, energy drops, hormones shift. Your body doesn't *want* to be at 12%.

3. **It requires the longest sustained consistency.** You can build up to 10 pull-ups in a few months of focused training. But fat loss at ~0.5kg/week means you're looking at 4-5 months of deficit *minimum*, and realistically longer because you won't be in a deficit every single week. Life happens — travel, stress, social eating.

4. **It conflicts with strength gains.** Building muscle (which helps pull-ups, push-ups) is easier in a slight surplus. Losing fat requires a deficit. You can do both as a relative beginner (body recomp), but it's slower than focusing on one.

That said — 12% is totally achievable, it just demands the most lifestyle change compared to the other goals which are pure training.

> DEVELOPER

what is like a good level of bodyfat that is considered atheletic and that you can see my abs

> AGENT

For visible abs, you're looking at roughly **15% or below**. That's where most guys start seeing at least a four-pack in good lighting.

- **15%**: Abs visible in good lighting, overall lean look. This is "athletic" by most standards.
- **12-13%**: Clear six-pack, visible vascularity in arms. Classic beach body look.
- **10%**: Very defined, veins everywhere. Hard to maintain long-term.

15% is a much more practical target than 12% — it's where you look noticeably fit, it's sustainable year-round, and your body doesn't fight you to stay there. Going from 24% to 15% means losing ~7kg of fat instead of ~9kg, and you avoid the hardest last few percent.

Honestly 15% might be the smarter goal to set as your intermediate target. Once you're there you can decide if you even want to push further.

> DEVELOPER

I want something that's easy to maintain and sixpack like bradpit fightclub

> AGENT

That's basically 12-13%. Brad Pitt in Fight Club was famously lean but not shredded — he was around 5-6% body fat at like 70kg, which is honestly not a look most people can or should maintain. He was also pretty underweight for his height.

A more sustainable version of that aesthetic — lean, defined six-pack, visible but not freaky — is around **12-13%**. So your original target of 12% is actually spot on.

The key difference from Pitt's look is he was very low bodyweight with minimal muscle mass. You at 12% with more muscle from calisthenics training would actually look better and healthier than that. The six-pack definition comes from the low body fat, not from having huge abs.

So stick with 12% as the long-term target. Just know that maintaining it requires staying on top of your diet permanently — it's not a "get there and relax" situation, but it's doable once it becomes habit. Plenty of people walk around at 12-13% year-round.

Perfect. Now I have a complete picture. Let me compile my findings:

## Comprehensive Security Analysis: Argus Telegram Bot User Identity & Data Isolation

### 1. **User Identification & Authorization**

The Telegram bot uses a **whitelist-based access control** defined in `config.py`:

```python
ALLOWED_USERS = {int(uid.strip()) for uid in os.environ.get("TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS", "").split(",") if uid.strip()}
```

**Key findings:**
- User identity is determined by `update.effective_user.id` (Telegram user ID)
- Authorization is enforced in `telegram_bot.py` via `is_authorized()` function
- The journal bot (`journal_bot.py`) uses the same `ALLOWED_USERS` whitelist
- There is **NO per-user tracking** of conversation state or data access

### 2. **Vault/Notes Access - CRITICAL SECURITY GAP**

The vault access is **completely unrestricted** across all authorized users:

**File access flow:**
- `tools.py:resolve_path()` - resolves paths to either `ARGUS_HOME` or `VAULT_PATH` without user filtering
- `tools.py:execute_tool()` - implements `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file` tools with **no user isolation**
- `tools.py:search_vault()` - uses grep across the entire vault (`str(VAULT_PATH)`) without filtering

**Vault access tools available:**
- `read_file` - reads from ARGUS_HOME or vault with path normalization only
- `search_vault` - full-text search across all vault files
- `list_files` - directory listing of vault
- `write_file`/`edit_file` - can create/modify vault files
- `git_push_vault` - commits and pushes all vault changes

**No per-user filtering exists.** Any authorized user gains access to:
- All task domain contexts
- All knowledge base files  
- All conversation history (through context system)
- All personal vault notes
- All journal entries

### 3. **Per-User Data Filtering - NONE FOUND**

**Conversation history (db.py):**
- Database schema has **no user_id column**:
```python
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    role TEXT NOT NULL,
    content TEXT NOT NULL,
    interface TEXT DEFAULT 'telegram',
    timestamp TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
)
```
- `get_history()` returns the last N messages **globally** without filtering
- `log_message()` stores messages without user attribution
- All conversation history is shared across any authorized user

**Context system exposure:**
- `context.py:build_chat_prompt()` - loads the same chat.md for all users
- `context.py:build_worker_prompt()` - loads CLAUDE.md, CONTEXT.md, and domain contracts for all users
- Domain contexts (chat/CONTEXT.md, knowledge/CONTEXT.md, etc.) contain no user-specific filtering
- The worker prompt includes task routing rules, which reference all projects

### 4. **System Prompt Data Exposure**

What appears in system prompts (potentially visible via audit/health checks):

**CLAUDE.md (worker layer 0):**
- Folder structure and workspace layout
- No personal data leak

**chat.md (chat model):**
- Project IDs with names: Personal (3), Argus (2), Research (4), **Project Pyari (5) marked as SHARED**, Inbox (1)
- References to "Sumeet" as the user
- Project routing information
- Task creation constraints and patterns

**domains/knowledge/CONTEXT.md:**
- References to "wiki/People" folder structure
- "My Thoughts" boundary reference (personal vault structure)
- References "references/second-brain.md" for vault structure

**Health check (`health.py:get_recent_conversations()`):**
- Returns last 40 messages from global conversation DB
- Includes all chat history without user filtering

**Audit (`audit.py:run_audit()`):**
- Dumps all system prompts, domain contracts, and references
- Includes folder structure and git status
- Exposes MCP configuration with allowed tools

### 5. **Journal Bot - SHARED BUT SEPARATED**

`journal_bot.py` has **separate token** from Argus bot but:
- Uses **same ALLOWED_USERS whitelist**
- Appends entries to date-stamped files in `VAULT_PATH/journal/`
- No user-specific journal folders
- Any authorized user can read/write all journal entries through vault access

### 6. **Tools Available & Their Scope**

**Personal data access tools (NO PER-USER FILTERING):**
- `get_strava` - fetches running/cycling data (single Strava account configured)
- `read_file`/`search_vault` - access entire vault
- `write_file`/`edit_file` - modify entire vault

**All task management (Vikunja via MCP):**
- `list_all_tasks`, `list_tasks`, `get_task`, `create_task`, `update_task`, `complete_task` - see all 5 projects including Personal and Project Pyari
- No per-user task filtering in MCP configuration

**System tools (expose system state):**
- `run_healthcheck` - exposes last 40 messages, infrastructure status, tool success rates
- `run_audit` - dumps all system architecture, prompts, and config
- `run_investigation` - captures recent conversations and board state

### 7. **MCP Client Configuration**

`mcp.json` exposes these task management tools for all authorized users:
```json
"allowed_tools": [
    "list_projects", "create_project", "update_project",
    "list_tasks", "list_all_tasks", "get_task",
    "create_task", "update_task", "complete_task", "delete_task",
    "move_task_to_project", "move_task_to_project_by_name",
    ... (33 tools total)
]
```

No filtering by user in the MCP layer.

### 8. **Critical Data Leak Scenarios - Adding a Second User**

**What a second authorized user WOULD see:**

1. **Entire conversation history** - all chat with first user (40-message sliding window in health checks)
2. **All personal vault notes** - wiki/, journal/, task contexts, personal knowledge base
3. **All tasks** - Personal (3), Argus (2), Research (4), **Project Pyari (5)**, Inbox (1)
4. **Strava fitness data** - run/cycling activities from the configured account
5. **System prompts** - revealing:
   - "Sumeet's mate" relationship
   - Personal project names and IDs
   - Knowledge base structure
   - Task management patterns and preferences
6. **Journal entries** - daily personal logs via vault access
7. **Recent audit/health snapshots** - captured at `/domains/validation/artifacts/`
8. **Investigation snapshots** - all bug reports with context

**Data NOT isolated:**
- Conversation history (not per-user in database)
- Vault access (no per-user restrictions)
- Task access (Vikunja has no per-user filtering in MCP layer)
- Strava credentials (shared account, no per-user data)
- System context (same prompts for all users)

---

## Summary

**The system is single-user by design with allowlisting, but has ZERO multi-user isolation.** If a second user is added to `ALLOWED_USERS`, they will have:

- Full read/write access to all vault files (personal notes, journal, task contexts)
- Full visibility of all conversation history
- Full access to all tasks across all projects (including personal)
- Full access to health checks and audit dumps
- Access to system prompts revealing personal information

**This is a critical security flaw.** The authorization layer only checks "is this user in the allowlist?" with no per-user data partitioning at the database, vault, or context level.

### Key File Paths:
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/config.py` - ALLOWED_USERS allowlist
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/db.py` - conversation DB with no user_id column
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/tools.py` - vault access with no filtering
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/telegram_bot.py` - auth check only
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/agent.py` - context and tool routing
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/prompts/chat.md` - reveals personal project info
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/CONTEXT.md` - routing rules
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/mcp.json` - MCP tools configuration
```

> AGENT

Yeah, there are a lot of issues. Right now Argus is completely single-user — adding Ashlyn to `ALLOWED_USERS` would basically give her full access to everything. Here's what would leak:

### The big problems

1. **Your entire Obsidian vault** — `read_file`, `search_vault`, `list_files` all operate on the full vault with no user filtering. She could ask "search my notes for..." and it'd search *your* notes.

2. **Conversation history** — the messages DB has no `user_id` column. It's one global conversation. She'd see your recent messages in context, and the bot would reference things you said to it.

3. **System prompts expose personal info** — `chat.md` references "Sumeet", your project names, routing rules. The health check dumps the last 40 messages. The audit tool dumps everything.

4. **Journal entries** — all in `vault/journal/` with no per-user separation. She could read your daily logs.

5. **Strava/fitness data** — single account, no filtering.

6. **Task manager** — Vikunja already supports multi-user (Ashlyn has an account), but the MCP layer doesn't filter by user. She'd see your Personal project, Research, etc.

### What's fine

- Vikunja itself already has Ashlyn as user 2 with Project Pyari shared — that part's actually ready for collaboration.

### What you'd need to fix for multi-user

- **Per-user conversation history** (add user_id to the DB)
- **Per-user vault access** (either separate vaults or a permissions layer)
- **Per-user context/system prompts** (different chat.md per user, strip personal info)
- **Tool access control** (restrict which tools each user can trigger — e.g., Ashlyn shouldn't have `search_vault`, `run_audit`, `run_healthcheck`)
- **Per-user Strava/fitness** (or just block those tools for her)

It's not a small lift. Want to think about what level of access she should actually have and we can scope it out?

> AGENT

OK here's the picture:

### GitHub — safe to invite, with one caveat

The `.env` file is gitignored and not tracked. No secrets in the repo. But the **code itself reveals personal info**:
- `chat.md` says "You are Sumeet's mate", references your vault structure, project names, personal task routing
- Domain contracts mention your name, project IDs, personal categories

That's not *secrets*, but it's your personal system prompt. If you're fine with Ashlyn seeing how Argus thinks about you (which honestly she probably already knows), then yeah, invite her. It's just code.

### Simplest multi-user approach

Don't try to make the whole system multi-tenant. Instead, **gate by user at the tool level**:

1. **Tag each message with the Telegram user ID** — add `user_id` to the messages DB so conversations don't bleed
2. **Create a user role config** — something like:
   ```python
   USER_ROLES = {
       SUMEET_ID: {"role": "admin", "tools": ALL_TOOLS},
       ASHLYN_ID: {"role": "member", "tools": ["create_task", "list_tasks", "complete_task", ...]}
   }
   ```
3. **Block sensitive tools for non-admin** — Ashlyn gets task management + shared features, but no `search_vault`, `read_file`, `run_audit`, `run_healthcheck`, `get_strava`, `git_push_vault`
4. **Separate system prompt per role** — admin gets `chat.md` as-is, member gets a stripped-down version without vault references or personal context
5. **Per-user conversation history** — filter `get_history()` by user_id so the bot doesn't leak your conversations into her context

That's basically 5 changes across `db.py`, `tools.py`, `telegram_bot.py`, `agent.py`/`context.py`, and a new `chat_member.md` prompt. No architectural overhaul needed.

The fitness stuff, Strava, journal, vault — all stay admin-only for now. You can selectively open things up later (like shared fitness goals if you two train together).

Want to start building this?

1	# Argus Worker
2	
3	You are the worker agent in a two-tier system. A fast chat model handles user conversation on Telegram and dispatches tasks to you. You receive a task description and intent, do the work using your tools, and return a clear result summary.
4	
5	You do NOT chat with the user directly — the chat model handles that. Focus on executing the task accurately and narrating your progress.
6	
7	## Folder Map
8	Your workspace is at the ARGUS_HOME directory:
9	
10	- `CONTEXT.md` — routing table: which domains exist and when to load them
11	- `domains/tasks/CONTEXT.md` — task management rules, priorities, project routing
12	- `domains/system/CONTEXT.md` — architecture, VPS services
13	- `domains/validation/CONTEXT.md` — health checks, audits
14	- `domains/knowledge/CONTEXT.md` — daily roundup, vault entity management, cross-linking
15	- `domains/chat/CONTEXT.md` — communication style (read for narration guidance)
16	- `references/` — stable reference material (architecture.md, telegram.md)
17	
18	Read the relevant domain contract with `read_file` before acting. Don't assume rules — check the contract first.
19

Perfect. I've read all three files in full. Here's my comprehensive analysis:

## 1. **config.py** — Configuration & User Management

**Environment Variables Loaded:**
- `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` — Required, no default
- `TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS` — CSV-formatted user IDs; parsed as a set of ints; empty string defaults to empty set
- `LITELLM_BASE_URL` — Default: `http://localhost:4000/v1`
- `LITELLM_API_KEY` — Default: `sk-dummy`
- `LITELLM_MODEL` — Default: `argus` (marked as legacy/unused)
- `CHAT_MODEL` — Default: `argus-chat`
- `WORKER_MODEL` — Default: `argus-worker`
- `VIKUNJA_API_URL` — Default: `http://localhost:3456/api/v1`
- `VIKUNJA_API_TOKEN` — Default: empty string
- `VAULT_PATH` — Default: `/root/codex`
- `ARGUS_HOME` — Default: parent directory of config.py
- `VIKUNJA_WEB_URL` — Default: `https://task.argbox.net`
- `OBSIDIAN_VAULT_NAME` — Default: `codex`
- `STRAVA_CLIENT_ID`, `STRAVA_CLIENT_SECRET`, `STRAVA_REFRESH_TOKEN` — Default: empty strings

**ALLOWED_USERS Logic:**
- Line 13: `ALLOWED_USERS = {int(uid.strip()) for uid in os.environ.get("TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS", "").split(",") if uid.strip()}`
- Parses comma-separated integers, creates a set of int user IDs
- If env var is empty or missing, results in empty set `{}`
- Empty set means **no restriction** (see telegram_bot.py line 49-50)

---

## 2. **telegram_bot.py** — Main Telegram Bot Layer

### User Identity Flow
- **Source:** `update.effective_user` (Telegram SDK object)
- **Fields extracted:**
  - `update.effective_user.id` — numeric Telegram user ID
  - `update.effective_user.username` — string username
- **Available in all handlers:** yes, via `update` parameter

### Authorization Check Function
```python
def is_authorized(update: Update) -> bool:
    if not ALLOWED_USERS:
        return True  # No restriction if set is empty
    return update.effective_user and update.effective_user.id in ALLOWED_USERS
```
- Called at start of every message/command handler
- Returns `True` if ALLOWED_USERS is empty OR user ID in set
- Returns `False` if ALLOWED_USERS is non-empty AND user ID not in set
- Logs warning when unauthorized (line 110, line 165)

### Message Dispatch Flow

**`handle_message(update, context)` — Lines 106-152:**
1. Check message exists and has text
2. Check `is_authorized(update)` → return silently if not
3. Log: `f"Message from {update.effective_user.username}: {user_msg[:100]}"`
4. Call `chat(user_msg, on_status=on_status)` via executor (blocking call wrapped in async)
5. On_status callback sends progress messages
6. Send reply via `update.message.reply_text()`

**User info passed to agent:**
- User message text only
- `on_status` callback for status updates
- **No user ID or username passed to agent** — agent receives only message text

### Command Handlers
All follow same pattern: check auth → execute → send reply

1. **`/start`** (line 155-158) — Greeting message
2. **`/fresh`** (line 161-166) — Clear conversation history + log username
3. **`/health`** (line 169-176) — Worker command: validation health check
4. **`/audit`** (line 179-186) — Worker command: architecture audit
5. **`/inv <description>`** (line 189-200) — Worker command: investigate issue
6. **`/roundup`** (line 203-211) — Worker command: daily roundup (silent=True)

### Worker Command Wrapper
**`_run_worker_command(update, user_command, task, intent, silent=False)` — Lines 62-103:**
- Wraps calls to `run_worker(task, intent, on_status=status_cb)`
- Manages "typing" indicator loop
- Sends status messages via `on_status` callback
- Silent mode suppresses initial status message
- **No user ID passed to worker**

---

## 3. **journal_bot.py** — Journal-Only Telegram Bot

### Design
Much simpler: logs raw messages to timestamped Obsidian journal files, commits to git.

### User Identity Handling
**Lines 55-56:**
```python
if ALLOWED_USERS and update.effective_user.id not in ALLOWED_USERS:
    return  # Silent rejection
```
- Same auth logic, but **silently fails** (no log, no response to user)
- `update.effective_user.id` is only user field checked
- **No username captured or logged**

### Message Flow
1. Check auth (silent fail if not authorized)
2. Extract raw text from `update.message.text`
3. Call `append_to_journal(text)` — writes to `JOURNAL_DIR/{YYYY-MM-DD}.md`
4. Git commit/push with timestamp
5. Reply "logged" to user

**Journal File Format:**
```markdown
# YYYY-MM-DD
**HH:MM** [message text]
```

---

## Key Findings: User Identity & Multi-User Considerations

### What IS Available
- User ID (integer)
- Username (string)
- All available in both bots

### What's NOT Passed to Agent Layer
- **telegram_bot.py:** Agent receives only message text + on_status callback
- **journal_bot.py:** No agent involved; direct file write
- **No per-user isolation or user ID routing**

### Where to Intercept for Per-User Logic

1. **In `handle_message()` (telegram_bot.py:106-152):**
   - User ID available: `update.effective_user.id`
   - Username available: `update.effective_user.username`
   - Could inject into `chat()` call or pass via context
   - Current: `chat(user_msg, on_status=on_status)` — no user param

2. **In `_run_worker_command()` (line 62):**
   - Could accept user ID param, pass to `run_worker()`
   - Current: no user info passed

3. **In `journal_bot.py handle_message()` (line 52-65):**
   - User ID available but not used
   - Could inject into `append_to_journal()` or filename pattern

4. **Config Level:**
   - ALLOWED_USERS is global set of IDs
   - No per-user config or routing rules
   - Could add `ALLOWED_USERS_CONFIG` dict with per-user settings

### Logging Availability
- **telegram_bot.py** logs username on every message: `f"Message from {update.effective_user.username}"`
- **journal_bot.py** does NOT log username: only `f"Logged: {text[:80]}"`
- Both have access to user ID and username at auth check point

---

## Summary: Complete User Flow

```
Telegram Message
    ↓
update.effective_user.id, .username available
    ↓
is_authorized(update) checks ID against ALLOWED_USERS set
    ↓
If authorized:
  - telegram_bot.py: chat(message_text) ← user ID/username NOT passed
  - journal_bot.py: append_to_journal(text) ← user ID/username available but ignored
    ↓
Agent layer has no knowledge of which user sent message
Conversation history is shared across all users
```

**Interception Points for Per-User Logic:**
- Line 141 in telegram_bot.py: modify `chat(user_msg, on_status)` to include user ID
- Line 96 in telegram_bot.py: modify `run_worker()` call to include user ID  
- Line 61 in journal_bot.py: pass user ID/username to `append_to_journal()`
- Agent layer would need to accept and use user_id parameter
```

Perfect. I have all four files. Let me now provide you with a comprehensive analysis of the message flow through the Argus bot system.

## Complete Message Flow Analysis

### 1. **agent.py** — Two-Tier Chat System with Tool Dispatch

**Key Function: `chat(user_message, on_status=None)`** (Line 147-226)
- **Entry point**: Takes raw `user_message` from Telegram
- **No user_id parameter**: The function does NOT receive or track user_id — it only stores messages generically
- **Flow**:
  1. Builds system prompt via `build_chat_prompt()`
  2. Loads last 20 messages of history via `get_history(20)` — no filtering by user_id
  3. Constructs messages array: [system_prompt] + history + new_user_message
  4. Logs user message via `log_message("user", user_message)` (generic, no user tracking)
  5. Enters tool-calling loop (up to 5 rounds)

**Tool Dispatch Logic** (Line 177-221):
- Separates tool calls into two categories:
  - **do_work**: Handoff to worker agent for multi-step tasks
  - **direct_calls**: Single-step tools executed by chat model immediately
- For direct calls: appends message → calls `_handle_tool_call()` → adds tool result to messages
- For do_work: calls `run_worker(task, intent, on_status)` → formats result via separate LLM call → logs as assistant

**Key Problem**: No user_id threading. All conversations store to a single global `messages` table.

**Helper Functions**:
- `_llm_call(model, messages, tools, max_tokens)`: Retry logic (3 attempts) for OpenAI-compatible API
- `_handle_tool_call(name, args)`: Routes to native tools or MCP server
- `_get_chat_tools()`: Returns native + MCP tools + DO_WORK_TOOL
- `run_worker(task, intent, on_status)`: Full ICM context, 30-round tool loop (line 74-115)

---

### 2. **context.py** — Prompt Assembly (No User Context)

**Two Prompt Builders**:

**`build_chat_prompt()`** (Line 21-25):
- Loads: `prompts/chat.md` (the personality rules)
- Adds: Current date/time via `_date_context()`
- **No user context, no personalization by user_id**

**`build_worker_prompt(task, intent)`** (Line 28-35):
- Loads full ICM context:
  - `CLAUDE.md` (worker agent instructions)
  - `CONTEXT.md` (routing table)
  - `domains/chat/CONTEXT.md` (communication style)
- Appends: Current date + task + intent
- **Task and intent are passed in, but no user_id**

**File Loading**: `load_file(path)` catches FileNotFoundError, returns empty string if missing

---

### 3. **db.py** — Conversation History (Global, No Multi-User Support)

**Schema** (Line 13-20):
```sql
CREATE TABLE messages (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    role TEXT NOT NULL,
    content TEXT NOT NULL,
    interface TEXT DEFAULT 'telegram',
    timestamp TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
)
```
**Critical Missing Field**: NO user_id or conversation_id column. All messages are stored in one flat table.

**Functions**:
- `log_message(role, content, interface='telegram')` (Line 25-29): Inserts one message. No user tracking.
- `get_history(limit=20)` (Line 32-38): Returns last `limit` messages ordered by id DESC, reversed. **Returns ALL messages across ALL users if using same database**.
- `clear_history()` (Line 41-45): Wipes entire table.

**Database Path**: `ARGUS_HOME / "data" / "conversations.db"`

---

### 4. **chat.md** — System Prompt (Full Contents)

The personality and tool instructions (lines 1-71):

**Core Personality Rules** (Lines 4-29):
- "Text like a real person, not an AI assistant"
- Short, casual, no emojis/em-dashes/exclamation marks
- NO robotic phrases like "operational" or "ready to execute"
- Be honest and encouraging about Sumeet's goals
- Examples of good vs bad responses to "hey how are you"

**Tools Available** (Lines 35-62):
- `list_all_tasks` — see all tasks
- `list_tasks / list_projects` — specific project filtering
- `create_task` — with project_id routing:
  - Personal (id=3)
  - Argus (id=2)
  - Research (id=4)
  - Project Pyari (id=5)
  - Inbox (id=1)
- `update_task / complete_task`
- `search_vault / read_file` — note lookup
- `do_work` — complex multi-step (epics, bulk ops)
- Link handling: weave URLs naturally in text

**Decision Rules** (Lines 64-70):
- One thing = use tool directly
- Many things = use do_work
- Just chatting = no tools
- **MANDATORY**: If message mentions "epic" or "subtasks", MUST use do_work

---

## Critical Findings: Message Flow Path

### Telegram → Agent → DB → LLM → Telegram

```
Telegram Bot (external)
    ↓ user_message
    ↓ (no user_id passed to chat())
    ↓
chat(user_message)
    ↓ log_message("user", user_message)
    ↓
get_history(20)  ← **FETCHES ALL LAST 20 MESSAGES FROM ALL USERS**
    ↓
build_chat_prompt()  ← **No user context injected**
    ↓
[CHAT MODEL LOOP]
    ├─ Direct tool calls
    │   ↓ _handle_tool_call()
    │   ↓ log_message("assistant", reply)
    └─ do_work handoff
        ↓ run_worker(task, intent)
           ├─ build_worker_prompt(task, intent)  ← **No user context**
           ├─ [WORKER LOOP] (30 rounds)
           ├─ Tool execution via MCP/native
           └─ log_message("assistant", formatted_result)
    ↓
return reply to Telegram
```

### The User ID Problem

**There is NO user_id threading anywhere:**
1. `chat()` doesn't receive user_id
2. `log_message()` has no user_id parameter
3. `get_history()` returns ALL messages from ALL users (single shared table)
4. Prompts are built without user context
5. Worker model has no user context either

This means **every Telegram user seeing the bot will get mixed conversation history** if multiple users are using the same bot instance. The database design is fundamentally single-user.

---

## Summary of Key Signatures

| Function | Signature | Key Parameters |
|----------|-----------|-----------------|
| **chat** | `chat(user_message, on_status=None)` | No user_id |
| **run_worker** | `run_worker(task, intent, on_status=None)` | No user_id |
| **build_chat_prompt** | `build_chat_prompt()` | No params, no user context |
| **build_worker_prompt** | `build_worker_prompt(task, intent)` | Task + intent only |
| **log_message** | `log_message(role, content, interface='telegram')` | No user_id |
| **get_history** | `get_history(limit=20)` | No user_id filter |
| **_handle_tool_call** | `_handle_tool_call(name, args)` | No user context |

**All conversation history is shared globally** — the system is not designed for multi-user scenarios.

10. **run_investigation** — Calls `inv.py` to capture bug snapshot. No details shown in code, but description mentions "recent conversations, tool logs, and board state". Likely exposes conversation history + logs + Vikunja board state.

## Implementation Plan: Multi-User Support for Argus

### Overview

The goal is to allow Ashlyn to use the Telegram bot for shared features (primarily task management on shared projects) while keeping Sumeet's personal data (vault, journal, Strava, conversation history, system diagnostics) completely isolated. The design uses a simple role-based approach: `admin` (Sumeet, full access) vs `member` (Ashlyn, restricted access).

---

### 1. User Role System in config.py

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/config.py`

Replace the flat `ALLOWED_USERS` set with a structured `USERS` dict that maps Telegram user IDs to role+metadata. Add a role-based permissions lookup.

**Current** (line 13):
```python
ALLOWED_USERS = {int(uid.strip()) for uid in os.environ.get("TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS", "").split(",") if uid.strip()}
```

**New design:**

```python
# User roles: admin gets full access, member gets restricted access
USERS = {}
for entry in os.environ.get("TELEGRAM_USERS", "").split(","):
    entry = entry.strip()
    if not entry:
        continue
    # Format: "telegram_id:role:name" e.g. "12345:admin:Sumeet,67890:member:Ashlyn"
    parts = entry.split(":")
    uid = int(parts[0])
    role = parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else "admin"
    name = parts[2] if len(parts) > 2 else "unknown"
    USERS[uid] = {"role": role, "name": name}

# Backward compat: keep ALLOWED_USERS for is_authorized checks
ALLOWED_USERS = set(USERS.keys())

# Tool permissions by role
ADMIN_ONLY_TOOLS = {
    "read_file", "write_file", "edit_file", "search_vault", "list_files",
    "get_strava", "run_healthcheck", "run_audit", "run_investigation",
    "git_push_vault",
}

# Vikunja projects visible per role (project IDs from Vikunja)
SHARED_PROJECTS = {5}  # Project Pyari
ADMIN_PROJECTS = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5}  # Inbox, Argus, Personal, Research, Project Pyari

# do_work intents allowed per role
ADMIN_ONLY_INTENTS = {"vault", "validation", "knowledge"}
```

**Env var change in `.env`:**
```
# Old: TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS=12345,67890
# New: TELEGRAM_USERS=12345:admin:Sumeet,67890:member:Ashlyn
```

Add a helper function to config.py:

```python
def get_user(telegram_id: int) -> dict | None:
    """Get user config by Telegram ID. Returns None if not authorized."""
    return USERS.get(telegram_id)
```

---

### 2. Per-User Conversation History

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/db.py`

**Schema migration:** Add a `user_id` column to the `messages` table. Existing messages get attributed to the admin by default.

Changes to `get_db()` (lines 10-20):

```python
def get_db():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(str(DB_PATH))
    conn.execute(
        """CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages (
            id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
            role TEXT NOT NULL,
            content TEXT NOT NULL,
            interface TEXT DEFAULT 'telegram',
            user_id INTEGER,
            timestamp TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
        )"""
    )
    # Migration: add user_id column if it doesn't exist
    columns = [row[1] for row in conn.execute("PRAGMA table_info(messages)").fetchall()]
    if "user_id" not in columns:
        conn.execute("ALTER TABLE messages ADD COLUMN user_id INTEGER")
    conn.commit()
    return conn
```

**Change `log_message` signature** (line 25):

```python
def log_message(role: str, content: str, interface: str = "telegram", user_id: int | None = None):
    conn = get_db()
    conn.execute(
        "INSERT INTO messages (role, content, interface, user_id) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)",
        (role, content, interface, user_id),
    )
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
```

**Change `get_history` signature** (line 32):

```python
def get_history(limit: int = 20, user_id: int | None = None) -> list[dict]:
    conn = get_db()
    if user_id is not None:
        rows = conn.execute(
            "SELECT role, content FROM messages WHERE user_id = ? ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ?",
            (user_id, limit),
        ).fetchall()
    else:
        rows = conn.execute(
            "SELECT role, content FROM messages ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ?", (limit,)
        ).fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return [{"role": r, "content": c} for r, c in reversed(rows)]
```

**Change `clear_history` signature** (line 40):

```python
def clear_history(user_id: int | None = None):
    conn = get_db()
    if user_id is not None:
        conn.execute("DELETE FROM messages WHERE user_id = ?", (user_id,))
    else:
        conn.execute("DELETE FROM messages")
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
```

---

### 3. Thread user_id Through telegram_bot.py to agent.py to db.py

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/telegram_bot.py`

The main threading change: extract `user_id` from `update.effective_user.id` at the Telegram entry point, pass it through to `chat()` and `run_worker()`, which pass it through to `log_message()` and `get_history()`.

**Change imports** (line 9):

```python
from .config import TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN, ALLOWED_USERS, USERS, get_user, log
```

**Change `_send_and_log`** (line 54):

```python
async def _send_and_log(update: Update, text: str, role: str = "assistant"):
    user_id = update.effective_user.id
    log_message(role, text, user_id=user_id)
    safe = _html_safe(text)
    for i in range(0, len(safe), 4096):
        await update.message.reply_text(safe[i : i + 4096], parse_mode="HTML")
```

**Change `handle_message`** (line 106): Pass `user_id` to `chat()`.

```python
async def handle_message(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE):
    if not update.message or not update.message.text:
        return
    if not is_authorized(update):
        log.warning(f"Unauthorized message from {update.effective_user.id}")
        return

    user_id = update.effective_user.id
    user_msg = update.message.text
    log.info(f"Message from {update.effective_user.username}: {user_msg[:100]}")

    # ... typing task setup unchanged ...

    try:
        reply = await loop.run_in_executor(
            None, lambda: chat(user_msg, user_id=user_id, on_status=on_status)
        )
    except Exception as e:
        log.error(f"Error: {e}", exc_info=True)
        reply = f"Something went wrong: {e}"
    finally:
        done.set()
        await typing_task

    safe = _html_safe(reply)
    for i in range(0, len(safe), 4096):
        await update.message.reply_text(safe[i : i + 4096], parse_mode="HTML")

### 4. Change agent.py to Accept user_id and Enforce Tool Gating

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/agent.py`

This is the most critical file. Both `chat()` and `run_worker()` need `user_id`, and both need to filter which tools are available based on user role.

**Change imports** (line 8):

```python
from .config import (
    ARGUS_HOME, CHAT_MODEL, WORKER_MODEL, llm, log,
    USERS, ADMIN_ONLY_TOOLS, ADMIN_ONLY_INTENTS, SHARED_PROJECTS,
)
```

**Add a helper to determine role:**

```python
def _user_role(user_id: int | None) -> str:
    """Return 'admin' for admin users or None, 'member' for restricted users."""
    if user_id is None:
        return "admin"  # internal/system calls default to admin
    user = USERS.get(user_id)
    return user["role"] if user else "member"
```

**Add tool filtering function:**

```python
def _filter_tools_for_role(tools: list[dict], role: str) -> list[dict]:
    """Remove admin-only tools for non-admin users."""
    if role == "admin":
        return tools
    return [t for t in tools if t["function"]["name"] not in ADMIN_ONLY_TOOLS]
```

**Change `_get_chat_tools`** to accept role:

```python
def _get_chat_tools(role: str = "admin") -> list[dict]:
    """Tools for the chat model, filtered by role."""
    all_tools = _get_all_tools()
    filtered = _filter_tools_for_role(all_tools, role)
    if role == "admin":
        return filtered + [DO_WORK_TOOL]
    # Members get do_work but with restricted intents (enforced at call time)
    member_do_work = {
        "type": "function",
        "function": {
            "name": "do_work",
            "description": "Complex multi-step work: epics with subtasks, bulk task operations.",
            "parameters": {
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {
                    "task": {"type": "string", "description": "What needs to be done"},
                    "intent": {"type": "string", "enum": ["tasks"]},
                },
                "required": ["task", "intent"],
            },
        },
    }
    return filtered + [member_do_work]
```

**Change `_handle_tool_call`** to enforce gating at execution time (defense in depth):

```python
def _handle_tool_call(name: str, args: dict, role: str = "admin") -> str:
    """Route tool call, with role-based gating."""
    if role != "admin" and name in ADMIN_ONLY_TOOLS:
        return f"Error: you don't have access to {name}."
    if mcp_client.is_mcp_tool(name):
        return mcp_client.call_tool(name, args)
    return execute_tool(name, args)
```

**Change `chat()` signature** (line 147):

```python
def chat(user_message: str, user_id: int | None = None, on_status: Callable[[str], None] | None = None) -> str:
    """Chat model: respond directly, use tools, or hand off to worker."""
    role = _user_role(user_id)
    system_prompt = build_chat_prompt(role=role)
    history = get_history(20, user_id=user_id)
    chat_tools = _get_chat_tools(role=role)

    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
    messages.extend(history)
    messages.append({"role": "user", "content": user_message})

    log_message("user", user_message, user_id=user_id)

    for _ in range(5):
        try:
            response = _llm_call(CHAT_MODEL, messages, chat_tools)
        except Exception as e:
            reply = f"Something went wrong: {e}"
            log_message("assistant", reply, user_id=user_id)
            return reply

        choice = response.choices[0]
        text = choice.message.content or ""

        if not choice.message.tool_calls:
            reply = _strip_handoff_json(text) or text or "(no response)"
            log_message("assistant", reply, user_id=user_id)
            return reply

        do_work_call = None
        direct_calls = []
        for tc in choice.message.tool_calls:
            if tc.function.name == "do_work":
                do_work_call = tc
            else:
                direct_calls.append(tc)

        if do_work_call:
            args = json.loads(do_work_call.function.arguments)
            task_desc = args.get("task", user_message)
            intent = args.get("intent", "tasks")

            # Enforce intent restrictions for non-admin
            if role != "admin" and intent in ADMIN_ONLY_INTENTS:
                reply = "I can't do that for you. That's restricted to admin users."
                log_message("assistant", reply, user_id=user_id)
                return reply

            log.info(f"Handoff to worker: intent={intent}, task={task_desc[:100]}")
            clean_text = _strip_handoff_json(text.strip())
            if clean_text and on_status:
                on_status(clean_text)

            worker_result = run_worker(task_desc, intent, user_id=user_id, on_status=on_status)
            log_message("assistant", f"[WORK COMPLETED] ...", user_id=user_id)

            # Format result (unchanged)
            try:
                fmt = _llm_call(CHAT_MODEL, [...], max_tokens=500)
                reply = fmt.choices[0].message.content or worker_result
            except Exception:
                reply = worker_result

            log_message("assistant", reply, user_id=user_id)
            return reply

        # Direct tool calls — pass role for gating
        messages.append(choice.message)
        for tc in direct_calls:
            args = json.loads(tc.function.arguments)
            log.info(f"Chat tool: {tc.function.name}({args})")
            result = _handle_tool_call(tc.function.name, args, role=role)
            messages.append({"role": "tool", "tool_call_id": tc.id, "content": result})

    reply = _strip_handoff_json(text) or "I couldn't complete that. Try rephrasing?"
    log_message("assistant", reply, user_id=user_id)
    return reply
```

    system_prompt = build_worker_prompt(task, intent, role=role)
    worker_tools = _filter_tools_for_role(_get_all_tools(), role)

    for _ in range(30):

### 5. Per-Role System Prompts in context.py

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/context.py`

**Change `build_chat_prompt`** to accept a role:

```python
def build_chat_prompt(role: str = "admin") -> str:
    """Build system prompt for the chat model, role-aware."""
    if role == "admin":
        chat_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "prompts" / "chat.md")
    else:
        chat_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "prompts" / "chat_member.md")
    parts = [chat_md, _date_context()]
    return "\n".join(parts)
```

**Change `build_worker_prompt`** to accept role:

```python
def build_worker_prompt(task: str, intent: str, role: str = "admin") -> str:
    """Build system prompt for the worker model, role-aware."""
    claude_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "CLAUDE.md")
    context_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "CONTEXT.md")
    chat_domain = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "domains" / "chat" / "CONTEXT.md")
    parts = [claude_md, "\n", context_md, "\n", chat_domain, _date_context()]
    if role != "admin":
        parts.append("\n## User Role\nThis user is a member (not admin). They can only manage tasks on shared projects. Do NOT access vault, journal, Strava, or any personal data.\n")
    parts.append(f"\n## Current Task\n{task}\nIntent: {intent}")
    return "\n".join(parts)
```

Proposed content for `chat_member.md`:

```markdown
# Argus

## HOW YOU TALK (this is the most important part)

You are Argus, a shared assistant on Telegram. You text like a real person, not an AI assistant. When someone says "hey how are you", you chat like a friend would. You don't mention tasks, tools, or work unless THEY bring it up first.

Rules:
- Talk like a person, not a bot. Short sentences. Casual.
- No emojis. No em dashes. No exclamation marks overuse.
- No swearing.
- No sycophancy or robotic phrases.
- Never describe yourself as "operational" or "ready to execute".
- When chatting casually, just chat. Don't steer to productivity.
- When they need something done, be direct and get it done.
- Be honest. If something's a bad idea, say so.

## WHO YOU ARE

Argus. A shared assistant on Telegram. You help with task management on shared projects.

## TOOLS

You have tools for managing tasks on shared projects.

**list_all_tasks**: See all tasks on shared projects.

**list_tasks / list_projects**: Check tasks in a specific project, or list all projects.

**create_task**: Create ONE task. ALWAYS set project_id. Don't set priority unless specified.

**NEVER invent details.** No made-up numbers, descriptions, deadlines, or context that weren't provided.

**Project routing** (use these IDs):
- **Project Pyari** (id=5): the shared project

**update_task / complete_task**: Change or finish ONE task.

**do_work**: Complex multi-step operations only. Epics with subtasks, bulk ops.

## LINKS

When a tool result contains a URL (like https://task.argbox.net/tasks/39), include it naturally in your response.

## SIMPLE RULE

One thing = use the tool directly.
Many things = use do_work.
Just chatting = no tools.

**MANDATORY: if the message mentions "epic", "subtasks", or asks for multiple related tasks, you MUST use do_work.**

## RESTRICTIONS

You only have access to shared task management. You cannot access notes, files, fitness data, or personal information.

### 6. Task Filtering for Vikunja MCP Tools

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/agent.py` (within `_handle_tool_call`)

For member-role users, task-related MCP tool calls need project filtering. The simplest approach: intercept MCP calls that have a `project_id` parameter and validate it, and filter results from `list_all_tasks`, `list_tasks`, `list_projects`.

Add to `_handle_tool_call`:

```python
def _handle_tool_call(name: str, args: dict, role: str = "admin") -> str:
    """Route tool call, with role-based gating."""
    if role != "admin" and name in ADMIN_ONLY_TOOLS:
        return f"Error: you don't have access to {name}."

    # For members, restrict Vikunja project access
    if role != "admin" and mcp_client.is_mcp_tool(name):
        return _mcp_call_with_project_filter(name, args)

    if mcp_client.is_mcp_tool(name):
        return mcp_client.call_tool(name, args)
    return execute_tool(name, args)
```

Then a separate helper:

```python
def _mcp_call_with_project_filter(name: str, args: dict) -> str:
    """Call MCP tool with project restrictions for members."""
    # Block creation/operations on non-shared projects
    if "project_id" in args and args["project_id"] not in SHARED_PROJECTS:
        return f"Error: you can only manage tasks on shared projects."

    result = mcp_client.call_tool(name, args)

    # For list operations, filter results to shared projects only
    if name in ("list_all_tasks", "list_tasks", "list_projects", "task_summary", "focus_now", "due_today", "overdue_tasks"):
        try:
            import json as _json
            data = _json.loads(result)
            if isinstance(data, list):
                filtered = [item for item in data if item.get("project_id") in SHARED_PROJECTS or item.get("id") in SHARED_PROJECTS]
                return _json.dumps(filtered, indent=2)
        except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError):
            pass  # Not JSON or unexpected format, return as-is

    return result
```

**Important edge case:** The `get_task` tool can retrieve any task by ID regardless of project. For members, after calling `get_task`, check the returned `project_id`:

```python
    if name == "get_task":
        result = mcp_client.call_tool(name, args)
        try:
            import json as _json
            data = _json.loads(result)
            if isinstance(data, dict) and data.get("project_id") not in SHARED_PROJECTS:
                return "Error: you can only view tasks on shared projects."
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            pass
        return result
```

**Note on practical approach:** The MCP tool results may not always be cleanly parseable JSON (some return formatted text). The initial implementation should focus on gating `create_task`, `update_task`, `complete_task`, `delete_task`, and `move_task_to_project` by checking `project_id` in the args. For listing tools, the system prompt in `chat_member.md` will guide the model to only query shared projects. The arg-level gating is the safety net; the prompt is the primary control.

### 9. Edge Cases and Safety Considerations

**Edge case: Ashlyn triggers `do_work`.**
Handled. The worker receives `role="member"` and gets a filtered tool list. The worker prompt includes a restriction notice. The intent is restricted to `"tasks"` only. Even if the LLM hallucinates a tool call for `search_vault`, the runtime check in `_handle_tool_call` blocks it.

**Edge case: Ashlyn asks about personal info conversationally.**
The member prompt (`chat_member.md`) has no references to vault, personal projects, or Sumeet's personal data. The model simply won't know about it. If it tries to use a restricted tool, the runtime gating blocks it.

**Edge case: health.py's `get_recent_conversations` dumps all messages.**
This is only called via `run_healthcheck`, which is admin-only both at the command handler level and the tool level. No change needed, but worth noting.

**Edge case: Shared conversation context leak.**
With per-user `user_id` on `get_history`, Ashlyn only sees her own conversation history. Sumeet only sees his. There is zero cross-contamination.

**Edge case: `assign_user` with Vikunja user IDs.**
The task domain contract (line 45 of `domains/tasks/CONTEXT.md`) already maps "Sumeet = 1, Ashlyn = 2". This works for both users. No change needed.

**Edge case: MCP tool result format varies.**
Some MCP tools return JSON, some return formatted text. The project filtering uses a try/except to handle both cases gracefully. If parsing fails, the result passes through -- the prompt-level guidance is the primary control, runtime filtering is defense-in-depth.

### 10. Implementation Sequence

1. **config.py** -- Add `USERS` dict, role constants, `ADMIN_ONLY_TOOLS`, `SHARED_PROJECTS`, `get_user()`. Update `.env` format.
2. **db.py** -- Add `user_id` column with migration, update `log_message`, `get_history`, `clear_history` signatures.
3. **context.py** -- Add `role` parameter to `build_chat_prompt` and `build_worker_prompt`.
4. **Create `prompts/chat_member.md`** -- Stripped-down prompt for member role.
5. **agent.py** -- Add `user_id` parameter to `chat()` and `run_worker()`, add `_user_role()`, `_filter_tools_for_role()`, `_mcp_call_with_project_filter()`, update `_handle_tool_call` with role gating.
6. **telegram_bot.py** -- Thread `user_id` through all handlers, add `_is_admin()`, gate admin-only commands, scope `/fresh` to user.
7. **journal_bot.py** -- Switch to `JOURNAL_ALLOWED_USERS` env var.
8. **Update `.env`** on VPS with new format.

Steps 1-4 can be done in any order. Step 5 depends on 1-3. Step 6 depends on 1-2 and 5. Step 7 is independent.

78	    tool_results: list[str] = []
79	
80	    messages = [
81	        {"role": "system", "content": system_prompt},
82	        {"role": "user", "content": task},
83	    ]
84	
85	    for _ in range(30):
86	        try:
87	            response = _llm_call(WORKER_MODEL, messages, worker_tools)
88	        except Exception as e:
89	            return f"Worker error: {e}"
90	
91	        choice = response.choices[0]
92	        text = choice.message.content or ""
93	
94	        if choice.finish_reason == "stop" and not choice.message.tool_calls:
95	            # Append collected URLs if the model didn't include them
96	            urls = _collect_urls(tool_results)
97	            if urls:
98	                missing = [u for u in urls if u not in text]
99	                if missing:
100	                    text = text.rstrip() + "\n\n" + "\n".join(missing)
101	            return text or "(worker produced no output)"
102	
103	        if text.strip() and on_status:
104	            on_status(text.strip())
105	
106	        messages.append(choice.message)
107	        if choice.message.tool_calls:
108	            for tc in choice.message.tool_calls:
109	                args = json.loads(tc.function.arguments)
110	                log.info(f"Worker tool: {tc.function.name}({args})")
111	                result = _handle_tool_call(tc.function.name, args)
112	                tool_results.append(result)
113	                messages.append({"role": "tool", "tool_call_id": tc.id, "content": result})
114	
115	    return "Worker hit the tool call limit."
116	
117	
118	# --- Chat model tools ---
119	
120	# Tools the chat model can use directly (simple single-step operations)
121	# Plus do_work for complex multi-step handoffs
122	DO_WORK_TOOL = {
123	    "type": "function",
124	    "function": {
125	        "name": "do_work",
126	        "description": "Complex multi-step work: epics with subtasks, bulk operations, health checks, audits.",
127	        "parameters": {
128	            "type": "object",
129	            "properties": {
130	                "task": {"type": "string", "description": "What needs to be done"},
131	                "intent": {"type": "string", "enum": ["tasks", "memory", "vault", "system", "validation", "knowledge"]},
132	            },
133	            "required": ["task", "intent"],
134	        },
135	    },
136	}
137	
138	
139	def _get_chat_tools() -> list[dict]:
140	    """Tools for the chat model: native + MCP + do_work."""
141	    all_tools = _get_all_tools()
142	    return all_tools + [DO_WORK_TOOL]
143	
144	
145	# --- Chat function ---
146	
147	def chat(user_message: str, on_status: Callable[[str], None] | None = None) -> str:
148	    """Chat model: respond directly, use tools, or hand off to worker."""
149	    system_prompt = build_chat_prompt()
150	    history = get_history(20)
151	    chat_tools = _get_chat_tools()
152	
153	    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
154	    messages.extend(history)
155	    messages.append({"role": "user", "content": user_message})
156	
157	    log_message("user", user_message)
158	
159	    # Chat model tool-calling loop (up to 5 rounds for simple multi-tool)
160	    for _ in range(5):
161	        try:
162	            response = _llm_call(CHAT_MODEL, messages, chat_tools)
163	        except Exception as e:
164	            reply = f"Something went wrong: {e}"
165	            log_message("assistant", reply)
166	            return reply
167	
168	        choice = response.choices[0]
169	        text = choice.message.content or ""
170	
171	        # No tool calls — pure chat response
172	        if not choice.message.tool_calls:
173	            reply = _strip_handoff_json(text) or text or "(no response)"
174	            log_message("assistant", reply)
175	            return reply
176	
177	        # Check if any tool call is do_work (handoff to worker)
178	        do_work_call = None
179	        direct_calls = []
180	        for tc in choice.message.tool_calls:
181	            if tc.function.name == "do_work":
182	                do_work_call = tc
183	            else:
184	                direct_calls.append(tc)
185	
186	        # Handle do_work handoff
187	        if do_work_call:
188	            args = json.loads(do_work_call.function.arguments)
189	            task_desc = args.get("task", user_message)
190	            intent = args.get("intent", "tasks")
191	            log.info(f"Handoff to worker: intent={intent}, task={task_desc[:100]}")
192	
193	            # Send acknowledgement
194	            clean_text = _strip_handoff_json(text.strip())
195	            if clean_text and on_status:
196	                on_status(clean_text)
197	
198	            # Run worker
199	            worker_result = run_worker(task_desc, intent, on_status=on_status)
200	            log_message("assistant", f"[WORK COMPLETED] Task: {task_desc}. Intent: {intent}. Result: {worker_result[:500]}")
201	
202	            # Format result
203	            try:
204	                fmt = _llm_call(CHAT_MODEL, [
205	                    {"role": "system", "content": "You are Argus. Format this into a brief Telegram message. Be concise. No emojis. No asterisks or markdown formatting. No em dashes. Plain text only. Include any URLs from the result naturally in the text (they will be auto-formatted as clickable links)."},
206	                    {"role": "user", "content": f"User asked: {user_message}\nWorker result: {worker_result}"},
207	                ], max_tokens=500)
208	                reply = fmt.choices[0].message.content or worker_result
209	            except Exception:
210	                reply = worker_result
211	
212	            log_message("assistant", reply)
213	            return reply
214	
215	        # Handle direct tool calls
216	        messages.append(choice.message)
217	        for tc in direct_calls:
218	            args = json.loads(tc.function.arguments)
219	            log.info(f"Chat tool: {tc.function.name}({args})")
220	            result = _handle_tool_call(tc.function.name, args)
221	            messages.append({"role": "tool", "tool_call_id": tc.id, "content": result})
222	
223	    # If we exhausted the loop, return last text
224	    reply = _strip_handoff_json(text) or "I couldn't complete that. Try rephrasing?"
225	    log_message("assistant", reply)
226	    return reply
227

1	"""Telegram bot interface."""
2	
3	import asyncio
4	import html
5	import re
6	from telegram import Update
7	from telegram.ext import ApplicationBuilder, MessageHandler, CommandHandler, filters, ContextTypes
8	
9	from .config import TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN, ALLOWED_USERS, log
10	from .agent import chat, run_worker
11	from .db import clear_history, log_message
12	
13	
14	def _prettify_url(m: re.Match) -> str:
15	    """Convert a raw URL into a nice clickable HTML link."""
16	    url = m.group(0)
17	    # Vikunja task links: show as #ID
18	    task_match = re.search(r'/tasks/(\d+)', url)
19	    if task_match:
20	        return f'<a href="{url}">#{task_match.group(1)}</a>'
21	    # Obsidian links: show as the note name
22	    obs_match = re.search(r'file=(.+)', url)
23	    if obs_match:
24	        from urllib.parse import unquote
25	        name = unquote(obs_match.group(1)).split("/")[-1]
26	        return f'<a href="{url}">{name}</a>'
27	    # Other URLs: show domain
28	    return f'<a href="{url}">{url}</a>'
29	
30	
31	def _html_safe(text: str) -> str:
32	    """Escape HTML, then convert raw URLs into clickable links."""
33	    # Extract existing <a> tags first
34	    links = []
35	    def save_link(m):
36	        links.append(m.group(0))
37	        return f"\x00LINK{len(links)-1}\x00"
38	    text = re.sub(r'<a\s[^>]*>.*?</a>', save_link, text, flags=re.DOTALL)
39	    text = html.escape(text)
40	    for i, link in enumerate(links):
41	        text = text.replace(f"\x00LINK{i}\x00", link)
42	    # Convert raw URLs to pretty links
43	    text = re.sub(r'https?://\S+', _prettify_url, text)
44	    text = re.sub(r'obsidian://\S+', _prettify_url, text)
45	    return text
46	
47	
48	def is_authorized(update: Update) -> bool:
49	    if not ALLOWED_USERS:
50	        return True
51	    return update.effective_user and update.effective_user.id in ALLOWED_USERS
52	
53	
54	async def _send_and_log(update: Update, text: str, role: str = "assistant"):
55	    """Send a message to Telegram AND log it to conversation DB."""
56	    log_message(role, text)
57	    safe = _html_safe(text)
58	    for i in range(0, len(safe), 4096):
59	        await update.message.reply_text(safe[i : i + 4096], parse_mode="HTML")
60	
61	
62	async def _run_worker_command(update: Update, user_command: str, task: str, intent: str, silent: bool = False):
63	    """Shared logic for worker commands. silent=True suppresses status messages."""
64	    log_message("user", user_command)
65	    if not silent:
66	        await _send_and_log(update, f"Running {intent}...")
67	
68	    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
69	    done = asyncio.Event()
70	
71	    async def keep_typing():
72	        while not done.is_set():
73	            try:
74	                await update.message.chat.send_action("typing")
75	            except Exception:
76	                pass
77	            try:
78	                await asyncio.wait_for(done.wait(), timeout=4)
79	                break
80	            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
81	                pass
82	
83	    typing_task = asyncio.create_task(keep_typing())
84	
85	    def on_status(text: str):
86	        try:
87	            future = asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(
88	                _send_and_log(update, text), loop)
89	            future.result(timeout=10)
90	        except Exception:
91	            pass
92	
93	    status_cb = None if silent else on_status
94	
95	    try:
96	        result = await loop.run_in_executor(None, lambda: run_worker(task, intent, on_status=status_cb))
97	    except Exception as e:
98	        result = f"Failed: {e}"
99	    finally:
100	        done.set()
101	        await typing_task
102	
103	    await _send_and_log(update, result)
104	
105	
106	async def handle_message(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE):
107	    if not update.message or not update.message.text:
108	        return
109	    if not is_authorized(update):
110	        log.warning(f"Unauthorized message from {update.effective_user.id} ({update.effective_user.username})")
111	        return
112	    user_msg = update.message.text
113	    log.info(f"Message from {update.effective_user.username}: {user_msg[:100]}")
114	
115	    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
116	    done = asyncio.Event()
117	
118	    async def keep_typing():
119	        while not done.is_set():
120	            try:
121	                await update.message.chat.send_action("typing")
122	            except Exception:
123	                pass
124	            try:
125	                await asyncio.wait_for(done.wait(), timeout=4)
126	                break
127	            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
128	                pass
129	
130	    typing_task = asyncio.create_task(keep_typing())
131	
132	    def on_status(text: str):
133	        try:
134	            future = asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(
135	                _send_and_log(update, text), loop)
136	            future.result(timeout=10)
137	        except Exception as e:
138	            log.warning(f"Failed to send status message: {e}")
139	
140	    try:
141	        reply = await loop.run_in_executor(None, lambda: chat(user_msg, on_status=on_status))
142	    except Exception as e:
143	        log.error(f"Error: {e}", exc_info=True)
144	        reply = f"Something went wrong: {e}"
145	    finally:
146	        done.set()
147	        await typing_task
148	
149	    # Final reply (already logged by chat() in agent.py)
150	    safe = _html_safe(reply)
151	    for i in range(0, len(safe), 4096):
152	        await update.message.reply_text(safe[i : i + 4096], parse_mode="HTML")
153	
154	
155	async def handle_start(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE):
156	    if not is_authorized(update):
157	        return
158	    await _send_and_log(update, "Hey, I'm Argus. What can I help with?")
159	
160	
161	async def handle_fresh(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE):
162	    if not is_authorized(update):
163	        return
164	    clear_history()
165	    log.info(f"Conversation history cleared by {update.effective_user.username}")
166	    await update.message.reply_text("Fresh start. Conversation history cleared.")
167	
168	
169	async def handle_health(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE):
170	    if not is_authorized(update):
171	        return
172	    await _run_worker_command(
173	        update, user_command="/health",
174	        task="Run a health check. Read domains/validation/health.md for instructions.",
175	        intent="validation",
176	    )
177	
178	
179	async def handle_audit(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE):
180	    if not is_authorized(update):
181	        return
182	    await _run_worker_command(
183	        update, user_command="/audit",
184	        task="Run an architecture audit. Read domains/validation/audit.md for instructions.",
185	        intent="validation",
186	    )
187	
188	
189	async def handle_inv(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE):
190	    if not is_authorized(update):
191	        return
192	    issue = update.message.text.replace("/inv", "").strip()
193	    if not issue:
194	        await _send_and_log(update, "What's the issue? Usage: /inv <description>")
195	        return
196	    await _run_worker_command(
197	        update, user_command=f"/inv {issue}",
198	        task=f"Investigate issue: {issue}. Read domains/validation/inv.md for instructions.",
199	        intent="validation",
200	    )
201	
202	
203	async def handle_roundup(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE):
204	    if not is_authorized(update):
205	        return
206	    await _run_worker_command(
207	        update, user_command="/roundup",
208	        task="Run the daily roundup. Read domains/knowledge/roundup.md for instructions.",
209	        intent="knowledge",
210	        silent=True,
211	    )
212	
213	
214	async def post_init(application):
215	    """Register bot commands so they show up in Telegram's / menu."""
216	    await application.bot.set_my_commands([
217	        ("fresh", "Clear conversation history and start fresh"),
218	        ("health", "Run system health check"),
219	        ("audit", "Review system architecture for issues"),
220	        ("inv", "Capture issue snapshot and create bug ticket"),
221	        ("roundup", "End-of-day journal processing"),
222	    ])
223	
224	
225	def run():
226	    app = ApplicationBuilder().token(TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN).post_init(post_init).build()
227	    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("start", handle_start))
228	    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("fresh", handle_fresh))
229	    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("health", handle_health))
230	    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("audit", handle_audit))
231	    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("inv", handle_inv))
232	    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("roundup", handle_roundup))
233	    app.add_handler(MessageHandler(filters.TEXT & ~filters.COMMAND, handle_message))
234	    log.info("Argus is running. Listening for messages...")
235	    app.run_polling()
236

1	"""Conversation history storage."""
2	
3	import sqlite3
4	from .config import ARGUS_HOME
5	
6	DB_PATH = ARGUS_HOME / "data" / "conversations.db"
7	DB_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
8	
9	
10	def get_db():
11	    conn = sqlite3.connect(str(DB_PATH))
12	    conn.execute(
13	        """CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages (
14	            id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
15	            role TEXT NOT NULL,
16	            content TEXT NOT NULL,
17	            interface TEXT DEFAULT 'telegram',
18	            timestamp TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
19	        )"""
20	    )
21	    conn.commit()
22	    return conn
23	
24	
25	def log_message(role: str, content: str, interface: str = "telegram"):
26	    conn = get_db()
27	    conn.execute("INSERT INTO messages (role, content, interface) VALUES (?, ?, ?)", (role, content, interface))
28	    conn.commit()
29	    conn.close()
30	
31	
32	def get_history(limit: int = 20) -> list[dict]:
33	    conn = get_db()
34	    rows = conn.execute(
35	        "SELECT role, content FROM messages ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ?", (limit,)
36	    ).fetchall()
37	    conn.close()
38	    return [{"role": r, "content": c} for r, c in reversed(rows)]
39	
40	
41	def clear_history():
42	    conn = get_db()
43	    conn.execute("DELETE FROM messages")
44	    conn.commit()
45	    conn.close()
46

1	"""Context loading — separate prompts for chat model and worker model."""
2	
3	from datetime import datetime
4	from pathlib import Path
5	
6	from .config import ARGUS_HOME
7	
8	
9	def load_file(path: Path) -> str:
10	    try:
11	        return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
12	    except FileNotFoundError:
13	        return ""
14	
15	
16	def _date_context() -> str:
17	    now = datetime.now()
18	    return f"\n## Current Date\nToday is {now.strftime('%A %d %B %Y')}. The time is {now.strftime('%H:%M')}.\n"
19	
20	
21	def build_chat_prompt() -> str:
22	    """Build system prompt for the fast chat model. No ICM, just personality + handoff rules."""
23	    chat_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "prompts" / "chat.md")
24	    parts = [chat_md, _date_context()]
25	    return "\n".join(parts)
26	
27	
28	def build_worker_prompt(task: str, intent: str) -> str:
29	    """Build system prompt for the worker model. Full ICM context."""
30	    claude_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "CLAUDE.md")
31	    context_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "CONTEXT.md")
32	    chat_domain = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "domains" / "chat" / "CONTEXT.md")
33	    parts = [claude_md, "\n", context_md, "\n", chat_domain, _date_context()]
34	    parts.append(f"\n## Current Task\n{task}\nIntent: {intent}")
35	    return "\n".join(parts)
36

10	TOOLS = [
11	    {
12	        "type": "function",
13	        "function": {
14	            "name": "read_file",
15	            "description": "Read a file. Use paths relative to the argus workspace (e.g. 'domains/tasks/CONTEXT.md') or relative to the vault root, or absolute paths.",
16	            "parameters": {
17	                "type": "object",
18	                "properties": {"path": {"type": "string"}},
19	                "required": ["path"],
20	            },
21	        },
22	    },
23	    {
24	        "type": "function",
25	        "function": {
26	            "name": "write_file",
27	            "description": "Create a NEW file or fully overwrite an existing one. For editing existing files, prefer edit_file instead.",
28	            "parameters": {
29	                "type": "object",
30	                "properties": {
31	                    "path": {"type": "string"},
32	                    "content": {"type": "string"},
33	                },
34	                "required": ["path", "content"],
35	            },
36	        },

41	            "name": "edit_file",
42	            "description": "Edit an existing file by replacing a specific string. Finds old_str in the file and replaces it with new_str. Fails safely if old_str is not found (no data loss). Use this instead of write_file when modifying existing files.",
43	            "parameters": {
44	                "type": "object",
45	                "properties": {
46	                    "path": {"type": "string", "description": "File path"},
47	                    "old_str": {"type": "string", "description": "Exact string to find in the file. Must match exactly including whitespace."},
48	                    "new_str": {"type": "string", "description": "String to replace it with."},
49	                },
50	                "required": ["path", "old_str", "new_str"],
51	            },
52	        },
53	    },
54	    {
55	        "type": "function",
56	        "function": {
57	            "name": "search_vault",
58	            "description": "Search the Obsidian vault for files containing a query string. Returns matching filenames and lines.",
59	            "parameters": {
60	                "type": "object",
61	                "properties": {"query": {"type": "string"}},
62	                "required": ["query"],
63	            },
64	        },
65	    },
66	    {
67	        "type": "function",
68	        "function": {
69	            "name": "list_files",
70	            "description": "List files in a directory within the Obsidian vault or Argus workspace.",
71	            "parameters": {
72	                "type": "object",
73	                "properties": {"directory": {"type": "string", "description": "Directory path. Use '.' for vault root."}},
74	                "required": ["directory"],
75	            },
76	        },
77	    },

79	        "type": "function",
80	        "function": {
81	            "name": "get_strava",
82	            "description": "Get running/cycling data from Strava. Use action 'latest' for the most recent activity with full detail (pace, splits, HR), 'recent' for a list of recent activities, or 'detail' with an activity_id for a specific activity.",
83	            "parameters": {
84	                "type": "object",
